3.5
.)

†
†
margin:

209

We turn to
\diamond
-adjointness.

†
†
margin:

II

Exercise
Show the following basic properties of
\diamond
-adjointness

1.

f^{\diamond}=g_{\diamond}

(
f
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
g
)
if and only if
f_{\diamond}=g^{\diamond}
.

2.

If
f
and
g
are
\diamond
-adjoint,
then
\IM f=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil
.

†
†
margin:

III

Exercise
Show in order:

1.

If
f
is
\diamond
-self-adjoint,
then
f\mathop{\circ}f
is
\diamond
-self-adjoint.

2.

If
f
is
\diamond
-positive,
then
f
is
\diamond
-self-adjoint.

3.

If
f
is
\diamond
-positive and
f\mathop{\circ}f
is pure,
then
f\mathop{\circ}f
is
\diamond
-positive.

†
†
margin:

IV

Lemma
Let
\alpha
be an isomorphism in a
\diamond
-effectus.
Then

1.

s\mathop{\circ}\alpha
is sharp for sharp predicates
s

and

2.

\alpha^{\diamond}(s)=s\mathop{\circ}\alpha

and
\alpha_{\diamond}(s)=s\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}

(so
\alpha
and
\alpha^{-1}
are
\diamond
-adjoint).

†
†
margin:

V

Proof
Let
s
be a sharp predicate. Then
s=\IM\pi_{s}
.
Note
\IM\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=s\mathop{\circ}\alpha

— indeed,
s\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=1

and when
p\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=1
,
we must have
p\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\geqslant s
,
which gives
p\geqslant s\mathop{\circ}\alpha
as desired.
So
s\mathop{\circ}\alpha
is indeed sharp.

So
\alpha^{\diamond}(s)=\left\lceil s\mathop{\circ}\alpha\right\rceil=s\mathop{\circ}\alpha

and
\alpha_{\diamond}(s)=\IM\alpha\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=s\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}

as promised.
\square

†
†
margin:

210

Definition
A map
f
in a
\diamond
-effectus is a
sharp map

provided
s\mathop{\circ}f
is sharp for all sharp predicates
s
.

†
†
margin:

II

Exercise
Show that the following are equivalent.

1.

f
is a sharp map.

2.

\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil=\left\lceil p\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}f

for every predicate
p
.

†
†
margin:

III

Example
In
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
the sharp maps are exactly the mni-maps
(i.e. the normal
*
-homomorphisms).
See
.

3.6

\&
-effectuses

†
†
margin:

211

In a
\diamond
-effectus
quotient and comprehension are not tied together by its axioms.
With two additional axioms, we will see quotient and comprehension
become tightly interwoven.

†
†
margin:

II

Definition
An
\&
-effectus
(‘‘andthen effectus’’)

is a
\diamond
-effectus
such that

1.

for each object
X

and each predicate
p
on
X
,
there is a unique
\diamond
-positive
map
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.75}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}}\colon X\to X

(“assert p”)
with
1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p
(see
3.5
)

and

2.

for every quotient
\xi\colon Y\to Z

and comprehension
\pi\colon X\to Y

the composite
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi
is pure.

In an
\&
-effectus,
we define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.75}p\mathbin{\&}q}\equiv q\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
,

pronounced “
p
andthen
q
”.
For brevity, we will write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.75}p^{2}}\equiv p\mathbin{\&}p
.

†
†
margin:

III

Remarks
The
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
maps are named after the
assert
statement
found in many programming languages.
The
assert
statement checks whether
the provided Boolean expression (i.e. predicate)
is true (at the time of execution) — and if it is, it will continue the program
without further action; if it isn’t, it will halt execution
immediately.
Our
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
maps can be thought of in the same way.
Alternatively,
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
can be viewed as a filter
that blocks states for which
p^{\perp}
holds.
With this intuition,
the predicate
p\mathbin{\&}q

corresponds to whether
p
and
q
are true,
by first checking
p
and then checking
q
.

The
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
maps can be used to construct more complicated measurements.
For instance, let
p,q,r
be three predicates (measurement outcomes)
on an object
X

with
p\ovee q\ovee r=1
.
Then
\left<\mathrm{asrt}_{p},\mathrm{asrt}_{q},\mathrm{asrt}_{r}\right>\colon X\to X+X+X

models measuring whether
p,q,r
hold
without discarding the state of the (possibly affected) system
after measurement
(in contrast to
\left<p,q,r\right>\colon X\to 1+1+1
).
If we discard the measurement outcome,
we find the map
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ovee\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ovee\mathrm{asrt}_{r}\colon X\to X
,
which is the side-effect of measuring
p,q,r
.

†
†
margin:

IIIa

The second axiom is closely related to polar decomposition:
in the case of
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
(flipping direction of arrows now),
the unique isomorphism
\varphi

such that
h\mathop{\circ}c=c^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}h^{\prime}

for the suitable standard filter
c^{\prime}
and corner
h^{\prime}

is given by
\mathrm{ad}_{u}

where
u
is the isomtry from the
polar decomposition
u\sqrt{\left\lceil h\right\rceil c(1)\left\lceil h\right\rceil}\equiv\sqrt{c(1)}\left\lceil h\right\rceil

restricted to a unitary.

†
†
margin:

IV

Examples
The category
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
is an
\&
-effectuses
with
\mathrm{asrt}_{a}\colon b\mapsto\sqrt{a}b\sqrt{a}
.
The first axiom is proven in

and the second in
.
The full subcategory
{\mathsf{CvN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
of commutative von Neumann algebras
is a
\&
-effectus as well.

The only other known example of an
\&
-effectus
is the category
{\mathsf{EJA}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
of Euclidean Jordan algebras
with positive unital maps in the opposite direction,
see
[
128
]
.

†
†
margin:

V

Proposition
For a predicate
p
in an
\&
-effectus the following are equivalent.

1.

p
is sharp,

2.

p\mathbin{\&}p=p
and

3.

\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
.

†
†
margin:

VI

Proof
First we prove that
p
is sharp if and only if
p\mathbin{\&}p=p.

So, assume
p
is sharp.
As
\diamond
-positive maps are
\diamond
-self-adjoint
we have
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\right\rceil=\left\lceil p\right\rceil=p
.
Thus
p\mathbin{\&}p=p\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p
.
For the converse, assume
p\mathbin{\&}p=p
.
From
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\right\rceil=\left\lceil p\right\rceil
,
we get
\left\lceil p\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p\mathbin{\&}\left\lceil p\right\rceil=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p
.
By assumption
p\mathbin{\&}p=p
.
So
p\mathbin{\&}(\left\lceil p\right\rceil\ominus p)=0
.
Hence
\left\lceil p\right\rceil\ominus p\leqslant\IMperp\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\left\lceil p\right\rceil^{\perp}
.
However
\left\lceil p\right\rceil\ominus p\leqslant\left\lceil p\right\rceil
.
Thus by
3.5

get
\left\lceil p\right\rceil\ominus p=0
. So
p
is indeed sharp.

Clearly, if
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
,
then
p=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p\mathbin{\&}p
.
It only remains to be shown
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

whenever
p
is sharp.
So assume
p
is sharp.
By definition
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
is pure:
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi

for some quotient
\xi
and comprehension
\pi
.
By assumption
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi
is pure as well,
so there is a quotient
\xi^{\prime}
and comprehension
\pi^{\prime}

with
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}
.
Note
1\mathop{\circ}\xi=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p

and
1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p\mathbin{\&}p=p=1\mathop{\circ}\xi
,
hence

1\mathop{\circ}\xi\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\pi\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi,

so
1\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}=1
. Thus
\xi^{\prime}
is an iso.
Also
(\IM\pi^{\prime})\mathop{\circ}\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=(\IM\pi^{\prime})\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}=1\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}=1

from which it follows
p=\IM\pi\leqslant(\IM\pi^{\prime})\mathop{\circ}\xi\leqslant 1\mathop{\circ}\xi=p
.
Thus
(\IM\pi^{\prime})\mathop{\circ}\xi=p=1\mathop{\circ}\xi
.
Hence
\IM\pi^{\prime}=1
and so
\pi^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.
Now we know
\pi\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}

is a comprehension and
\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi
is a quotient,
we see
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
is pure.
As
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
is
\diamond
-self-adjoint,
we see
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
is
\diamond
-positive.
By uniqueness of positive maps
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
,
as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

VII

Proposition
Let
C
be an
\&
-effectus and
s

be any sharp predicate.
There exist comprehension
\pi_{s}
of
s

and quotient
\zeta_{s}
of
s^{\perp}
such that

\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\ =\ \mathrm{id}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{s}.

In fact, for every comprehension
\pi
of
s
,
there is a quotient
\xi
of
s^{\perp}

with
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=\mathrm{id}
and
\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}

and
conversely for every quotient
\xi
of
s^{\perp}

there exists a comprehension
\pi
of
s

with
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=\mathrm{id}
and
\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}
.

†
†
margin:

VIII

Proof
Let
s
be any sharp predicate.
By definition
\diamond
-positive maps are pure and so
there is a quotient
\xi
and comprehension
\pi

with
\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}
.
So

\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{s}\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{sharp-prop}}}}}{{=}}\ \mathrm{asrt}_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{s}\ =\ \pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi.

Thus
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=\mathrm{id}
.
We compute
s=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{s}=1\mathop{\circ}\xi

and so

\displaystyle\IM\pi

\displaystyle\ =\ \IM\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi

as
\xi\mathop{\circ}\pi=\mathrm{id}

\displaystyle\ =\ \IM\mathrm{asrt}_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi

by dfn.
\xi
and
\pi

\displaystyle\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{s})_{\diamond}(\IM\pi)

by dfn.
(\ )_{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{s})^{\diamond}(\IM\pi)

by
\diamond
-s.a.
\mathrm{asrt}_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil(\IM\pi)\mathop{\circ}\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\right\rceil

by dfn.
(\ )^{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\xi\right\rceil

\displaystyle\ =\ s.

Thus
\pi
is a comprehension of
s

and
\xi
is a quotient of
s^{\perp}
.
We have proven the first part.

For the second part,
let
\pi^{\prime}
be any comprehension of
s
.
Then
\pi^{\prime}=\pi\mathop{\circ}\alpha
for some iso
\alpha
.
Define
\xi^{\prime}=\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\xi
.
It is easy to see
\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}

and
\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}=\mathrm{id}
.
The other statement is proven in a similar way.
\square

†
†
margin:

IX

Notation
In an
\&
-effectus
together with chosen comprehension
\pi_{s}
for
s
,
we will write
\zeta_{s}

for the unique quotient for
s^{\perp}

satisfying
\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=\mathrm{id}

and
\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}
.
We call this
\zeta_{s}

the
corresponding quotient
of
\pi_{s}

and vice versa
\pi_{s}
the
corresponding comprehension

of
\zeta_{s}
.

†
†
margin:

X

Warning

\xi_{s^{\perp}}=\zeta_{s}
!

†
†
margin:

XI

Proposition
In an
\&
-effectus
both comprehensions and pure maps are closed under composition.

†
†
margin:

XII

Proof
We will first prove that comprehensions are closed under composition.
Assume
\pi_{1}\colon X\to Y

and
\pi_{2}\colon Y\to Z

are comprehensions with
s=\IM\pi_{1}

and
t=\IM\pi_{2}
.
We will show
\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}

is a comprehension for
\IM\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}
.
To this end, let
f\colon V\to Z
be any map
with
(\IM\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1})^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}f=0
.
As
\IM\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}\leqslant\IM\pi_{2}=t

we get
t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}f=0
,
so
f=\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}g_{2}
for a unique
g_{2}\colon V\to Y
.
Let
\zeta_{2}
be a quotient for
t^{\perp}

such that
\zeta_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{2}=\mathrm{id}
,
which exists by
3.6
.
Then
s\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}=s\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}=1

so
s\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{2}\geqslant\IM\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}
.
Consequently

s\mathop{\circ}g_{2}\ =\ s\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}g_{2}\ =\ s\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{2}\mathop{\circ}f\ \geqslant\ (\IM\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1})\mathop{\circ}f\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}f\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}g_{2}.

Hence there exists a unique
g_{1}\colon V\to X

with
\pi_{1}\mathop{\circ}g_{1}=g_{2}

and so
\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}\mathop{\circ}g_{1}=f
.
By monicity of
\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}
,
this
g_{1}
is unique and so
\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{1}

is indeed a comprehension.

†
†
margin:

XIII

Now we will prove that pure maps are closed under composition.
Assume
g\colon X\to Y
and
f\colon Y\to Z
are pure maps.
Say
f=\pi_{1}\mathop{\circ}\xi_{1}

and
g=\pi_{2}\mathop{\circ}\xi_{2}

for some comprehensions
\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}

and quotients
\xi_{1}
,
\xi_{2}
.
By definition of
\&
-effectus
there is a comprehension
\pi^{\prime}
and quotient
\xi^{\prime}

such that
\xi_{1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{2}=\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}
.
By the previous point
\pi_{1}\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}

is a comprehension
and
\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi_{2}
is a quotient by
3.3
.
Thus

f\mathop{\circ}g=\pi_{1}\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\xi_{2}

is pure.
\square

†
†
margin:

XIV

Exercise
Show that in an
\&
-effectus,
we have
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}p}
.

†
†
margin:

XV

Exercise
Show that in an
\&
-effectus,
we have

\displaystyle\IM f\leqslant s\quad

\displaystyle\iff\quad\mathrm{asrt}_{s}\mathop{\circ}f=f

\displaystyle 1\mathop{\circ}f\leqslant t\quad

\displaystyle\iff\quad f\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t}=f

for any sharp predicates
s
and
t
.

†
†
margin:

XVI

Definition
Let
C
be an
\&
-effectus.
In
3.6
we saw pure maps are closed under composition.
Write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.75}\Pure C}
for the subcategory
of pure maps.

†
†
margin:

212

Lemma
In an
\&
-effectus,

\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

is a quotient for
p^{\perp}
.

†
†
margin:

II

Proof
Recall
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\right\rceil=\left\lceil p\right\rceil
and so

\IM\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ (\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil})_{\diamond}(\left\lceil p\right\rceil)\ =\ \IM\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\ =\ \IM\mathrm{id}\ =\ 1,

where
\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}
is the comprehension corresponding to
\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}
.
By
3.6

\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi

for some comprehension
\pi
and quotient
\xi
.
Putting it together:

1=\IM\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\IM\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi\leqslant\IM\pi
,
so
\IM\pi=1
and thus
\pi
is an isomorphism,
hence
\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

is a quotient for the orthocomplement of
\left\lceil p\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p
.
\square

†
†
margin:

III

Proposition
Every map
f

in an
\&
-effectus
factors as

f\ =\ \pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}g\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

(3.10)

for a unique total and faithful map
g
.
Furthermore the following holds.

1.

If
f
is pure, then
g
is an isomorphism.

2.

If
f
is pure and
\IM f=1
, then
f
is a quotient.

3.

If
f
is pure and
1\mathop{\circ}f=1
, then
f
is a comprehension.

†
†
margin:

IV

Proof
By the universal property of
\pi_{\IM f}
,
there is a unique
g^{\prime}
with
f=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}
.
To show
g^{\prime}
is faithful, assume
p\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}=0

for some predicate
p
.
Then
0=p\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}=p\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}=p\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}f

and so
p\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}\leqslant\IMperp f
,
hence
p=p\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\IM f}\leqslant(\IMperp f)\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\IM f}=0
, which
shows
g^{\prime}
is indeed faithful.

Note
1\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}=1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}g^{\prime}=1\mathop{\circ}f
.
By
3.3
and
3.6

there is a unique total
g
with
g^{\prime}=g\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}
.
Clearly (
3.10
) holds
and
g
is the unique map
for which (
3.10
) holds
as comprehensions are mono and quotients are epi.
As
1=\IM g^{\prime}=\IM g\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\leqslant\IM g
,
we see
\IM g=1
and so
g
is faithful.

To prove point 1, assume
f
is pure.
That is:
f=\pi\mathop{\circ}\xi
for some comprehension
\pi

and quotient
\xi
.
As
1\mathop{\circ}\pi=1
and
\IM\xi=1
,
we see
\IM\pi=\IM f
and
1\mathop{\circ}\xi=1\mathop{\circ}f
.
Thus
\pi=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha

and
\xi=\beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

for some isomorphisms
\alpha
and
\beta
.
Thus
f=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

and so by uniqueness of
g
,
we see
g=\alpha\mathop{\circ}\beta
is an isomorphism.

To prove point 2, additionally assume
\IM f=1
.
Then
\pi_{\IM f}
is an iso and so
using the previous, we see
f
is indeed a quotient.
Point 3 is just as easy.
\square

†
†
margin:

213

Proposition
If
C
is an
\&
-effectus,
then
(\Scal C)^{\mathsf{op}}
is an effect divisoid,
see
3.2.4
.

†
†
margin:

II

Proof
Let
\lambda,\mu
be scalars with
\lambda\leqslant\mu
.
Recall that the scalar
1=\mathrm{id}

and so
\mu=1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\mu}=\mathrm{asrt}_{\mu}
.
By
3.6
,
there is a unique
\lambda^{\prime}

with
\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu=\lambda
.
Define
\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\lambda$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}=\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}
.

For the moment, assume
\lambda=\mu
.
Then
\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu=\mu=1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu

as
\IM\mu=\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{\mu}=\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil
.
By epicity of
\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu
,
we see
\lambda^{\prime}=1
, hence
\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}=\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil
.
As we have
\mu\leqslant\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil
and
\left\lceil\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil\right\rceil=\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil
,
we see axioms 2 and 3 hold.

We return to the general case to prove axiom 1.
Clearly
\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\lambda$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}=\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\leqslant 1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}=\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil=\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}

and
\mu\odot_{\mathsf{op}}\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\lambda$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}=\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu=\lambda

as required.
Assume
\sigma
is an arbitrary
scalar with
\mu\odot_{\mathsf{op}}\sigma=\lambda

and
\sigma\leqslant\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}\equiv\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil
.
Then
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}

for a unique
\sigma^{\prime}
.
As
\lambda^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu=\lambda=\sigma^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil\mu\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu

we must have
\lambda^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}
, whence
\reflectbox{$\nicefrac{{\reflectbox{$\lambda$}}}{{\reflectbox{$\mu$}}}$}=\sigma
.
\square

†
†
margin:

III

Lemma
In an
\&
-effectus, if
s\perp t

for sharp
s,t
, then
s\mathbin{\&}t=0=t\mathbin{\&}s
.

†
†
margin:

IV

Proof
Write
r\equiv(s\ovee t)^{\perp}
.
As
1=s\ovee t\ovee r
and
s\mathbin{\&}s=s
, we have

s\ =\ s\mathbin{\&}1\ =\ s\mathbin{\&}s\ovee s\mathbin{\&}t\ovee s\mathbin{\&}r\ =\ s\ovee s\mathbin{\&}t\ovee s\mathbin{\&}r

and so
s\mathbin{\&}t\leqslant s\mathbin{\&}t\ovee s\mathbin{\&}r=0
.
\square

†
†
margin:

p\ \leqslant\ s\quad\iff\quad s\mathbin{\&}p\ =\ p

for sharp
s
and any predicate
p
.

VI

Exercise
Show that in a
\&
-effectus,
a map
\left<f,g\right>

is sharp if and only if both

and

are sharp.
(Hint: use
3.5
and
3.4.1
.)

3.7

\dagger
-effectuses

†
†
margin:

214

Definition
A
\dagger
-category
(‘‘dagger category’’)

is a category
C
together with an involutive identity-on-objects
functor
(\ )^{\dagger}\colon C\to{C}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}

—
that is, for all objects
X
and maps
f,g
in
C
, we have

1.

(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}

2.

\mathrm{id}^{\dagger}=\mathrm{id}

3.

f^{\dagger\dagger}=f

and

4.

X^{\dagger}=X
.

Cf.
[
14
,
102
,
49
,
50
]
.
In any
\dagger
-category we may define the following.

1.

An endomap
f
is called
\dagger
-self-adjoint

if
f^{\dagger}=f
.

2.

An endomap
f
is
\dagger
-positive

if
f=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}g
for some other map
g
.

3.

An isomorphism
\alpha
is called
\dagger
-unitary

whenever
\alpha^{-1}=\alpha^{\dagger}
.

†
†
margin:

II

Example
The category
\mathsf{Hilb}

of Hilbert spaces with bounded linear maps
is a
\dagger
-category
with the familiar adjoint as
\dagger
.

†
†
margin:

215

Definition
We call an
\&
-effectus
C

a
\dagger
-effectus
(‘‘dagger effectus’’) provided

1.

\Pure C
is a
\dagger
-category
satisfying
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

and
f
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
f^{\dagger}
;

2.

for every
\dagger
-positive
f
,
there is a unique
\dagger
-positive
g

with
g\mathop{\circ}g=f

and

3.

\diamond
-positive maps are
\dagger
-positive.

†
†
margin:

II

Examples
In
3.7

we will see that the category
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
is a
\dagger
-effectus.
Recently we have shown with van de Wetering
that the category
{\mathsf{EJA}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}

of Euclidean Jordan algebras
with positive unital maps in the opposite direction,
is a
\dagger
-effectus as well.
[
128
]

†
†
margin:

III

Theorem
An
\&
-effectus
is a
\dagger
-effectus if and only if

1.

for every predicate
p
, there is a unique predicate
q

with
q\mathbin{\&}q=p
;

2.

\mathrm{asrt}^{2}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}^{2}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

for all predicates
p,q

and

3.

a quotient for a sharp predicate (e.g.
\zeta_{s}
)
is a sharp map, see
3.5
.

†
†
margin:

IV

Proof
Necessity will be proven in

3.7

and sufficiency in
3.7
.
\square

†
†
margin:

V

Especially the sufficiency requires quite some preparation.
For convenience, call
C
a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus

if
C
is an
\&
-effectus
satisfying axioms 1, 2 and 3 from the Theorem above.

†
†
margin:

VI

Corollary
The category
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
of von Neumann algebras with
ncpu-maps in the opposite direction,
which is an
\&
-effectus by
3.6
,
is also a
\dagger
-effectus.

†
†
margin:

VIa

Remarks
The dagger on the pure maps of
\mathsf{vN}
(that exists by the previous
corollary) is fixed by the following two rules.

1.

For any (nmiu-)isomorphism
\vartheta

we have
\vartheta^{\dagger}=\vartheta^{-1}

as
\vartheta
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\vartheta^{-1}

and pure maps are rigid, see
.

2.

The standard filter
c\colon\mathscr{A}\to\left\lceil b\right\rceil\mathscr{A}\left\lceil b\right\rceil

for
b\in\mathscr{A}

(ie.
c(a)=\sqrt{b}a\sqrt{b}
, see
2.6
)
has as dagger
c^{\dagger}\colon\left\lceil b\right\rceil\mathscr{A}\left\lceil b\right\rceil\to\mathscr{A}

given by
c^{\dagger}(a)=\sqrt{b}a\sqrt{b}
.
(This follows from
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

and
3.7
.)

More concretely,
for any pure ncp-map
\varphi\colon\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{B}

we can find
a unique isomorphism
\vartheta\colon\left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil\mathscr{A}\left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil\to\left\lceil\varphi(1)\right\rceil\mathscr{B}\left\lceil\varphi(1)\right\rceil

such that

\varphi(a)\ =\ \sqrt{\varphi(1)}\,\vartheta\bigl(\left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil a\left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil\bigr)\,\sqrt{\varphi(1)}\qquad\text{(for all $a\in\mathscr{A}$)}

(this follows from
and
)
and so by the previous two rules we get

\varphi^{\dagger}(b)\ =\ \left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil\,\vartheta^{-1}\Bigl(\sqrt{\varphi(1)}\,b\,\sqrt{\varphi(1)}\Bigr)\,\left\lceil\varphi\right\rceil\qquad\text{(for all $b\in\mathscr{B}$)}.

†
†
margin:

VIIb

In special cases, we can give a simpler definition of the dagger.
For instance, if the
pure map is of the form
\varphi\colon\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})\to\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{K})
,
then
\varphi=\mathrm{ad}_{T}
for some operator
T\colon\mathscr{K}\to\mathscr{H}

and in that case
\varphi^{\dagger}=\mathrm{ad}_{T^{*}}
.

Another example is a pure map
\varphi\colon\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{B}

between finite-dimensional von Neumann algebras.
The Hilbert–Schmidt inner product
[a,b]\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}a^{*}b

turns a finite-dimensional von Neumann algebra into a Hilbert space.
The adjoint of
\varphi
with respect to this inner product
equals
\varphi^{\dagger}
.
This can be seen by considering the special cases
of nmiu-isomorphisms and standard filters
(noting nmiu-isomorphisms are trace-preserving).

†
†
margin:

VII

Remarks
In an
\&
-effectus
with separating predicates
(see
3.2.3
),
the second axiom of a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus is equivalent
to

(p\mathbin{\&}q)^{2}\mathbin{\&}r\ =\ p\mathbin{\&}(q^{2}\mathbin{\&}(p\mathbin{\&}r))

for all predicates
p,q,r
.
This is essentially the
fundamental formula
of quadratic Jordan algebras

[
89
, §4.2]

(with
U_{x}y\equiv x^{2}\mathbin{\&}y
).

The second axiom of a
\dagger
-effectus
has been considered before in
\dagger
-categories
by Selinger
[
103
]

who calls it the
unique square root axiom
.

†
†
margin:

216

Lemma
In a
\dagger
-effectus,
a map is
\dagger
-positive if and only if it is
\diamond
-positive.

†
†
margin:

II

Proof
Assume
f
is
\dagger
-positive.
By assumption 2, there is a (unique)
\dagger
-positive

g
with
f=g\mathop{\circ}g
.
By
\dagger
-positivity of
g
,
there is an
h
with
g=h^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}h
.
Using the fact
h
is
\diamond
-adjoint
to
h^{\dagger}
, we see
g
is
\diamond
-self-adjoint:

g^{\diamond}\ =\ (h^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}h)^{\diamond}\ =\ h^{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}(h^{\dagger})^{\diamond}\ =\ (h^{\dagger})_{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}h_{\diamond}\ =\ (h^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}h)_{\diamond}\ =\ g_{\diamond}.

Thus
f
is the square of the
\diamond
-self-adjoint map
g
,
hence
f
is
\diamond
-positive.
\square

†
†
margin:

III

Lemma
Predicates in a
\dagger
-effectus have a unique square root:
for every predicate
p
,
there is a unique predicate
q

with
q\mathbin{\&}q=p
.

†
†
margin:

IV

Proof
Let
p\colon X\to 1
be any predicate.
By assumption 3,
the
\diamond
-positive map
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

is also
\dagger
-positive.
So by assumption 2,
there is (a unique)
\dagger
-positive map
f

with
f\mathop{\circ}f=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
.
Define
q=1\mathop{\circ}f
.
By
3.7

f
is
\diamond
-positive
and so by uniqueness of
\diamond
-positive maps,
we get
f=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\equiv\mathrm{asrt}_{q}
.
We compute

q\mathbin{\&}q\ =\ q\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ \equiv\ 1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}f\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ p,

which shows
p
has as square root
q
.

To show uniqueness, assume
p=r\mathbin{\&}r
for some
predicate
r
.
Note

\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{r\mathbin{\&}r}\ \overset{\smash{\textsf{\ref{andthen-square-rule}}}}{=}\ \mathrm{asrt}_{r}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{r}.

As
\mathrm{asrt}_{r}
is
\diamond
-positive,
it is also
\dagger
-positive by the third axiom.
So by the second axiom
\mathrm{asrt}_{r}=\mathrm{asrt}_{q}
.
Thus
r=q
, which shows uniqueness of the square root.

\square

†
†
margin:

V

Proposition
In an
\&
-effectus with square roots
(e.g.
\dagger
- or
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus) we have

\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\ =\ \alpha\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\alpha}

for every isomorphism
\alpha
and predicate
p
.

†
†
margin:

VI

Proof
There is some
q
with
q\mathbin{\&}q=p
.
The map
\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\alpha

is
\diamond
-self-adjoint:

\displaystyle(\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\alpha)_{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ =\ \alpha^{-1}_{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}(\mathrm{asrt}_{q})_{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}\alpha_{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{iso-diamond-adjoint}}}}{=}\ \alpha^{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}(\mathrm{asrt}_{q})^{\diamond}\mathop{\circ}(\alpha^{-1})^{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ =\ (\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\alpha)^{\diamond}.

Thus
\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\alpha=\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\alpha
is
\diamond
-positive. By uniqueness of
\diamond
-positive maps,
we get
\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\alpha=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\alpha}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\alpha}
.
Postcomposing
\alpha
, we find
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\alpha=\alpha\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\alpha}
, as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

VII

Proposition
In a
\dagger
-effectus:
\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}=\pi_{s}

(recall convention
3.6
for
\pi_{s}
and
\zeta_{s}
).

†
†
margin:

VIII

Proof
As
\pi_{s}
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\pi_{s}^{\dagger}
,
we have

\IM\pi_{s}^{\dagger}\ =\ (\pi^{\dagger}_{s})_{\diamond}(1)\ =\ (\pi_{s})^{\diamond}(1)\ =\ \left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\right\rceil\ =\ 1

and similarly
\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}^{\dagger}\right\rceil=\IM\pi_{s}=s
.
As
1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}^{\dagger}\leqslant\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}^{\dagger}\right\rceil=s
,
there is some pure
h
with
\pi_{s}^{\dagger}=h\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
.
By
3.6
,
there is also some pure and faithful
g

with
\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}=\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}g
.
Using
\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=\mathrm{id}
twice we find

\mathrm{id}\ =\ \mathrm{id}^{\dagger}\ =\ \pi_{s}^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}\ =\ h\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}g\ =\ h\mathop{\circ}g.

(3.11)

Now
1=1\mathop{\circ}h\mathop{\circ}g\leqslant 1\mathop{\circ}g
and so
g
is total.
Clearly
\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
is
\dagger
-positive,
hence
\diamond
-positive and so by uniqueness of
\diamond
-positive maps:

\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}g\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{s}\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}.

So by epicity of
\zeta_{s}
, we find
\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}=\pi_{s}
, as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

IX

Corollary
In a
\dagger
-effectus,

\pi_{s}
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\zeta_{s}
.
Also
\alpha^{\dagger}=\alpha^{-1}

for any iso
\alpha
.

†
†
margin:

X

Exercise
Show that in an
\&
-effectus
where every predicate has a square root
and where
\pi_{s}
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\zeta_{s}

(e.g. a
\dagger
-effectus)
we have

\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\ =\ \zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}}
.
(Hint: mimic the proof of
3.7
.)

†
†
margin:

XI

Theorem
A
\dagger
-effectus is a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus.

†
†
margin:

XII

Proof
Assume
C
is a
\dagger
-effectus.
Axiom 1 is already proven in
3.7
.

†
†
margin:

XIII

(Ax. 2)
Let
p,q
be predicates.
To start, note
1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=p\mathbin{\&}q
and

\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{q})_{\diamond}(p)\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{q})^{\diamond}(p)\ =\ \left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil.

So using
3.6

we know

\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}

for some iso
\alpha
.
Applying the dagger to both sides
we get, using
3.7
and
3.7
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}\mathop{\circ}\zeta^{\dagger}_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\pi^{\dagger}_{\left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}.

Combining both:

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}^{2}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

\displaystyle\qquad=\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil q\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}

\displaystyle\qquad=\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}

\displaystyle\qquad=\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{asrt-absorp-rule}}}}}{=}\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathbin{\&}q}^{2},

as desired.

†
†
margin:

XIV

(Ax. 3)
Pick any sharp predicates
s,t
.
We want to show
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
is sharp.
To this end, we will show
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

is the image of
\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}
.
Clearly
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}=1
.
Let
p
be any sharp predicate with
p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}=1
.
Then
p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\geqslant\IM\pi_{t}=t
.
So
p^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\leqslant t^{\perp}

and hence
\left\lceil p^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\right\rceil\leqslant t^{\perp}
.
As
\pi_{s}
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\zeta_{s}
by
3.7
,
we get
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\leqslant\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\right\rceil\leqslant p
,
which shows
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

is the image of
\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}

and consequently sharp.
\square

†
†
margin:

217

Let
f
be a pure map in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus.
We will work towards the definition of
f^{\dagger}
.
By
3.6
we
know there is an iso
\alpha
with

f\ =\ \pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}.

In a
\dagger
-effectus
we have
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
,

\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}=\pi_{s}
,

\alpha^{\dagger}=\alpha^{-1}

and
\pi_{s}^{\dagger}=\zeta_{s}

(for corresponding
\zeta_{s}
and
\pi_{s}
)
—
so we are forced to define

f^{\dagger}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f},

(3.12)

where
\zeta_{\IM f}

is the unique corresponding
quotient of
\pi_{\IM f}

and
\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}

the unique corresponding comprehension of
and
\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}
,
see
3.6
.
Before we declare (
3.12
) a definition,
we have to check whether it is independent
of choice of
\pi
(and corresponding
\zeta
).
So suppose
f=\pi^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

for some iso
\alpha^{\prime}
, comprehension
\pi^{\prime}
of
\IM f

and quotient
\zeta^{\prime}
of
\smash{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil^{\perp}}
.
There are isos
\beta
and
\gamma

such that
\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\beta

and
\zeta^{\prime}=\gamma\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}
.
We will take a moment to relate
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
:
as
\pi_{\IM f}
is mono
and
\zeta_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

is epic,
we have
\beta\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\gamma=\alpha

and so
(\alpha^{\prime})^{-1}=\gamma\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\beta
.
To continue,
it is easy to see
\beta^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}

is the unique corresponding quotient to
\pi^{\prime}

and
\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\gamma^{-1}

is the unique corresponding comprehension to
\zeta^{\prime}
.
So with this choice of quotient and comprehension,
we are forced to define

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\gamma^{-1}\mathop{\circ}{\alpha^{\prime}}^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\beta^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\gamma^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\gamma\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\beta^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f},

which is indeed consistent with (
3.12
).
So we are justified to declare:

†
†
margin:

II

Definition
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus, for a pure map
f
, define

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.75}f^{\dagger}}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f},

where
\alpha
is the unique iso such that

f=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}
.

†
†
margin:

III

Exercise
Show that in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

\displaystyle\pi_{s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\zeta_{s}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}

\displaystyle\alpha^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{-1}

for a quotient
\zeta_{s}
corresponding to
\pi_{s}

and iso
\alpha
.

†
†
margin:

218

To compute
f^{\dagger\dagger}
,
we need to put
f^{\dagger}

in the standard form of
3.6
.
To do this, we need to pull
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}
from one side to the other.
In this section we will work towards a general result for this.

†
†
margin:

II

Lemma
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus,

\pi_{s}
is
\diamond
-adjoint to
\zeta_{s}
.

†
†
margin:

III

Proof
Pick any sharp
t,u
.
We have to
show
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\leqslant u^{\perp}

if and only if
u\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\leqslant t^{\perp}
.
So assume
t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\leqslant u^{\perp}
.
Then
t=t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\leqslant u^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}=(u\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s})^{\perp}

so
u\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\leqslant t^{\perp}
.
For the converse, assume
u\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\leqslant t^{\perp}
.
Then
u\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{s}=u\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\leqslant t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
.
From this,
the

\diamond
-self-adjointness of
\mathrm{asrt}_{s}

and the fact that
t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
is sharp,
we find
(t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s})^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\leqslant u^{\perp}

and so

\displaystyle t\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ (t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{id})^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ (t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s})^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ (t^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s})^{\perp}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ u^{\perp},

as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

IV

Definition
In an
\&
-effectus, a map
f
is
pristine

if it is pure and
1\mathop{\circ}f
is sharp.

†
†
margin:

V

Remarks
In general, pristine maps are not closed under composition:
in
{\mathsf{vN}}{{}^{\mathsf{op}}}
, the maps
\mathrm{asrt}_{\left|0\right>\!\left<0\right|}

and
\mathrm{asrt}_{\left|+\right>\!\left<+\right|}
on
M_{2}

are both pristine,
but their composite is not.
However, with a non-standard composition
of pristine maps
(namely
f\cdot g\equiv\mathrm{asrt}_{\left\lfloor 1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}g\right\rfloor}\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}g
),
the pristine maps are closed under composition and even
form a
\dagger
-category
[
23
]
.
Essentially the same construction
has been found independently by
[
53
]

for partial isometries between Hilbert spaces.

†
†
margin:

VI

Exercise
Show that in an
\&
-effectus, every pristine map
h
is of the form

h\ =\ \pi_{\IM h}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{1\mathop{\circ}h}

for some iso
\alpha
.

†
†
margin:

VII

Proposition
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus,
with pristine map
h
, we have

\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}h\ =\ h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}h}

for any predicate
p
with
p\leqslant\IM h
.

†
†
margin:

VIII

Proof
For brevity,
write
t=1\mathop{\circ}h

and
s=\IM h
.
By
3.7

there is some iso
\alpha

such that
h=\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}
.
Note
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\left\lceil p\right\rceil\leqslant\left\lceil s\right\rceil=s

and so
\mathrm{asrt}_{p}=\mathrm{asrt}_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

by the first rule of
3.6
.
By the second rule of
3.6

and
1\mathop{\circ}p=p\leqslant s
,
we see
p=p\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{s}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{s}}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}

by
3.7

and
3.7

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}}.

Putting everything together

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}h

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

by
3.7

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}}

by
3.7

and
3.7

\displaystyle\ =\ h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}h},

as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

IX

Exercise
Working in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus, show in order

1.

if
h\equiv\pi_{\IM h}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{1\mathop{\circ}h}

is some pristine map,
then
h^{\dagger}=\pi_{1\mathop{\circ}h}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM h}
;

2.

h^{\dagger\dagger}=h
for any pristine
h
;

3.

h^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}h=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}h}
for any pristine map
h
;

4.

p\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}\leqslant\IM h
for any predicate
p

and pristine map
h

and

5.

if
p\leqslant 1\mathop{\circ}h
,
then
\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}}\mathop{\circ}h=h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}

for any pristine map
h
.

†
†
margin:

X

Proposition
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus,
for every pure map
f
,
there exists a unique pristine map
h

with
1\mathop{\circ}h=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil

and
f=h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}
.
Furthermore
f^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}
.

†
†
margin:

XI

Proof
By
3.6

we know
f=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

for some iso
\alpha
.
Define
h=\pi_{\IM f}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}
.
Clearly
1\mathop{\circ}h=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil

and
f=h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}
.
Also

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ \equiv\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\IM f}

by
3.6

\displaystyle\ \equiv\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}.

Only uniqueness remains.
Assume
f=h^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

for some pristine
map
h^{\prime}
with
\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}h^{\prime}\right\rceil=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil
.
By
3.6
and
3.6
,
there is an iso
\alpha^{\prime}

with
h^{\prime}=\pi_{\IM h}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}
.
As
\zeta_{\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

is a quotient (by
3.6
),
quotients are faithful and
f=h^{\prime}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}
,
we see
\IM h^{\prime}=\IM f

and so
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
by
3.6
.
\square

†
†
margin:

XII

Proposition
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus, we have
f^{\dagger\dagger}=f

for any pure map
f
.

†
†
margin:

XIII

Proof
By
3.7

we have
f=h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}

and
f^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}

for some pristine
h

with
1\mathop{\circ}h=\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil
.
Clearly
1\mathop{\circ}f\leqslant\left\lceil 1\mathop{\circ}f\right\rceil=1\mathop{\circ}h=\IM h^{\dagger}
,
so by
3.7

we get
f^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}=h^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}}
.
Consequently

f^{\dagger\dagger}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}}\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger\dagger}\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{asrt-pristine-reverse}}}{=}\ \mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f\mathop{\circ}h^{\dagger}}\mathop{\circ}h\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{asrt-pristine-reverse}}}{=}\ h\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{1\mathop{\circ}f}\ =\ f,

as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

219

Now we tackle the most tedious part
of
3.7
:
we will show
(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}

in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus.
To avoid too much repetition, let us fix the setting.

†
†
margin:

II

Setting
Let
f,g
be two composable pure maps in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus.
For brevity, write
p=1\mathop{\circ}f
,
q=1\mathop{\circ}g
,

s=\IM f
and
t=\IM g
.
Let
\varphi
and
\psi

be the unique isomorphisms (see
3.6
) such that

f\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\qquad\text{and}\qquad g\ =\ \pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}.

Define
h=\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

and
k=\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}
.
To compute
(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}
,
we have to put
f\mathop{\circ}g

in the standard form
of
3.6
.
We will do this step-by-step,
first we put
\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}

in standard form.

\displaystyle 1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil p\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{asrt-absorp-rule}}}{=}\ p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}

\displaystyle\IM\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ (\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p})_{\diamond}(t)\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{quotcompr-diamond-adjoint}}}{=}\ \left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\right\rceil.

Thus there is a unique isomorphism
\chi
with

\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\ =\ \pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}.

(3.13)

Next, we consider
\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}
, clearly

1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ =\ p\mathop{\circ}k\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ =\ p\mathop{\circ}g.

Concerning the image, first
note
p\mathop{\circ}k=p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\leqslant 1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}=\left\lceil q\right\rceil

and so we must have
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\leqslant\left\lceil q\right\rceil
, which implies

\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ =\ (\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k})_{\diamond}(q)\ =\ \left\lceil\left\lceil q\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\right\rceil\ =\ \left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil.

So there is a unique isomorphism
\omega
such that

\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\ =\ \pi_{\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\omega\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}.

(3.14)

Next, we consider
\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}
.
Note
\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}

is a quotient for a sharp predicate,
hence sharp and so
\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}=\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\rceil=\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\rceil

by
3.5
.
As quotients are closed under composition
(
3.3
),
there is an iso
\beta

with

\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\ =\ \beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}.

(3.15)

Finally, we deal with
\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}
.
By
3.6
this is again a comprehension.
We compute

\displaystyle\IM\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ (\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil})_{\diamond}(1)

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{quotcompr-diamond-adjoint}}}}}{=}\ \zeta_{s}^{\diamond}(\varphi_{\diamond}(\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil))

\displaystyle\ =\ \lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil\mathop{\circ}\varphi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{sharp-ceil}}}}}{=}\ \lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\varphi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}\rceil

\displaystyle\ =\ \lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\rceil

and so there must be a unique iso
\alpha
with

\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}=\pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha.

(3.16)

†
†
margin:

III

Lemma
In setting
3.7
, we have

f\mathop{\circ}g=\pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\omega\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}
.

†
†
margin:

IV

Proof
It’s a long, but easy verification,
either with a diagram

\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{asrt}_{q}}

\scriptstyle\omega\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}

\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}}
\textstyle{\bullet}
(
3.14
)
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}}
3.7
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi}
(
3.16
)
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}}
(
3.15
)
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}}
(
3.13
)
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle\pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha

\scriptstyle{\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}}

or with equational reasoning

\displaystyle f\mathop{\circ}g

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\eqref{dagger-iso-chi}}}}{=}\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{pristine-asrt}}}}}{=}\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\eqref{dagger-iso-beta}}}}{=}\ \pi_{s}\mathop{\circ}\varphi\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\eqref{dagger-iso-alpha}}}}{=}\ \pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{q}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\eqref{dagger-iso-omega}}}}{=}\ \pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\omega\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta\mathop{\circ}\omega\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g},

whichever the Reader might prefer.
\square

†
†
margin:

V

Corollary

(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\omega^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\beta^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}
.

†
†
margin:

VI

To show
(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}
,
it is sufficient to proof that
the ‘daggered’ version
of each of the subdiagrams
(
3.16
),
(
3.15
),
(
3.13
),
(
3.14
) and

3.7
of the above diagram holds.
We start with the simple ones.

†
†
margin:

VII

Lemma
In setting
3.7
,
the daggered version of
(
3.15
) holds — that is:

\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta^{-1}\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}.

†
†
margin:

VIII

Proof
The map
\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta

is a comprehension for
\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil
— indeed

\displaystyle(\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta)_{\diamond}(1)

\displaystyle\ =\ (\zeta^{\diamond}_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{\diamond})(\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil)

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi\right\rceil

\displaystyle\ =\ \left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil.

Furthermore

\displaystyle\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta

\displaystyle\qquad\ \overset{\mathclap{\eqref{dagger-iso-beta}}}{=}\ \beta^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \mathrm{id}.

So
\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta
is the unique comprehension corresponding to
\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}
— that is:
\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\right\rceil}
,
as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

IX

Exercise
Show that in the setting
3.7
,
the daggered version of
(
3.16
) holds — i.e.

\alpha^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil t\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}\right\rceil}\ =\ \zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\varphi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}.

(Hint: mimic the proof of
3.7
.)

†
†
margin:

X

Exercise
Show that in the setting
3.7
,
we have

\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}.

(This is the daggered version of the subdiagram
marked
3.7
.)

†
†
margin:

XI

Proposition
If in a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus,

\nu
is the unique iso (cf.
3.7
) such that

\mathrm{asrt}_{a}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{b}\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{b\mathbin{\&}a},

then
\mathrm{asrt}_{b}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}
.

†
†
margin:

XII

Proof
Let
\mu
be the unique iso with
\mathrm{asrt}_{b}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a}\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{{a\mathbin{\&}b}}
.
We will see
\mu=\nu^{-1}
.
For brevity, write

\displaystyle\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}

\displaystyle\ =\ (a\mathbin{\&}b)\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}

\displaystyle\ =\ (b\mathbin{\&}a)\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}

By
3.7
and
3.6
, we have

\pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a\mathbin{\&}b}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{a\mathbin{\&}b}.

Now the second axiom of a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus comes into play

\displaystyle\pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}^{2}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{a\mathbin{\&}b}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{a}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}^{2}_{b}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}.

Thus as quotients are epis and comprehensions are monos:

\mathrm{asrt}^{2}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\ =\ \nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}.

(3.17)

We want to show
\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}
is an epi.
First note

\left\lceil\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}\right\rceil\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{sharp-ceil}}}{=}\ \left\lceil\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\right\rceil\ \overset{\textsf{\ref{asrt-absorp-rule}}}{=}\ \left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}

and so
\IM\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}=\left\lceil\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}\right\rceil=1
,
which tells us
\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}

is a quotient and therefore an epi.
So, from (
3.17
) we get

\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}=\nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\mu
and so

\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\ =\ 1\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\mu\ =\ \overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}\mu.

(3.18)

Using this equation again in the previous, we find

\nu\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}\ =\ \nu\mathop{\circ}\mu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}\mu}\ =\ \nu\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}}\mathop{\circ}\mu\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{\overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}}.

Thus
\nu\mathop{\circ}\mu=\mathrm{id}
and so
\mu=\nu^{-1}
.
Write
l=\pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}
. Then

\displaystyle(a\mathbin{\&}b)\mathop{\circ}l^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ =\ (a\mathbin{\&}b)\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}

by
3.7

\displaystyle\ =\ \overline{a\mathbin{\&}b}\mathop{\circ}\nu\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \overline{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}

by (
3.18
) and
\mu=\nu^{-1}

\displaystyle\ =\ b\mathbin{\&}a.

And so, keeping in mind
a\mathbin{\&}b\leqslant\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil=1\mathop{\circ}l
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{b}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a}

\displaystyle\ =\ l\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{a\mathbin{\&}b}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{(a\mathbin{\&}b)\mathop{\circ}l^{\dagger}}\mathop{\circ}l

by
3.7

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}l

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{b\mathbin{\&}a}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil b\mathbin{\&}a\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\nu^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil a\mathbin{\&}b\right\rceil},

as promised.
\square

†
†
margin:

XIII

Corollary
In setting
3.7
,
the daggered version of
(
3.14
) holds — that is:

\mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}k}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}g}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}g\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\omega^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\lceil p\mathop{\circ}k\rceil}.

†
†
margin:

XIV

Lemma
In setting
3.7
,
the daggered version of
(
3.13
) holds — that is:

\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}.

†
†
margin:

XV

Proof
The heavy lifting has been done in
3.7
already. To start, note

\displaystyle\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\mathclap{\smash{\textsf{\ref{asrt-absorp-rule}}}}}{=}\ \mathrm{asrt}_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\mathclap{\smash{\eqref{dagger-iso-chi}}}}{=}\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\mathclap{\smash{\textsf{\ref{pristine-asrt}}}}}{=}\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t\mathbin{\&}p}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}t\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha_{2}\mathop{\circ}\chi\mathop{\circ}\beta_{2}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil t\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t\mathbin{\&}p},

where
\alpha_{2}
and
\beta_{2}
are the unique isomorphisms such that

\displaystyle\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}t\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\ =\ \beta_{2}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil t\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}.

With the same reasoning as in
3.7

and
3.7
, we see

\displaystyle\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \alpha_{2}^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}t\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \pi_{\left\lceil t\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta_{2}^{-1}.

Now we can apply
3.7
:

\displaystyle\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{asrt-absorp-rule}}}}}{=}\ \zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ \overset{\smash{\mathclap{\textsf{\ref{dagger-iso-mu}}}}}{=}\ \zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t\mathbin{\&}p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil t\mathbin{\&}p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\beta_{2}^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\alpha_{2}^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\mathbin{\&}t\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{t\mathbin{\&}p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\mathop{\circ}\pi_{t}\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\chi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil},

as desired.
\square

†
†
margin:

XVI

Proposition
In a
\dagger^{\prime}
-effectus
(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}

holds.

†
†
margin:

Proof
We work in setting
3.7
.

(f\mathop{\circ}g)^{\dagger}=g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}

3.7

\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\zeta_{\lceil t\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\rceil}}

or alternatively by

\displaystyle g^{\dagger}\mathop{\circ}f^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ =\ \mathrm{asrt}_{q}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil q\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\psi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{t}\mathop{\circ}\mathrm{asrt}_{p}\mathop{\circ}\pi_{\left\lceil p\right\rceil}\mathop{\circ}\varphi^{-1}\mathop{\circ}\zeta_{s}
